# Sources for the open readings

The texts behind the roadmap's T2 and T3 items, one topic per file, with the
Tibetan as printed and its content restated in English (SPEC §8: facts in
our own words). How to read them is in [PLAN.md](PLAN.md), and how several
are read at once, a subagent each, in [PROTOCOL.md](PROTOCOL.md);
what is still to be read, and why, in the work plan of
[ROADMAP.md](../ROADMAP.md) (T2). The layout the pool grows into (a corpus file
per source chapter, claims, question files, passage ids) and the index that
reads it, `tools/sources/kb.py`, are in [STRUCTURE.md](STRUCTURE.md).

RD92 read 2026-10-31: the 暦林問答集's eight year gods (§6, §8–§15, with §31, §46, §54) by eye on GR and MS: 大歳, 大陰, 歳破 by the branch as today; 歳刑 a whole quarter by the triad; no anchor for the 大將軍's 仲, no rule for 歳殺, 黄幡 'the grave of the 大歳'; the 大將軍's quarter-wide ban and five-day 遊行; compared with the NDL's 『日本の暦』 and Wikipedia's table (koyomi8 and the NAOJ have none). RD94 read 2026-10-31: 天一神's direction and 日遊神 by eye on the 暦林問答集 (§43, §53), the 協紀辨方書 (1741) and 古事類苑 方技部's quotations (簾中抄, 拾芥抄, 頭書長暦, 貞丈雜記): no 方違え in the 暦林問答集, the 方違え as one night elsewhere, 日遊神's outside course only in the 協紀辨方書 (= 鶴神's = 天一's); the digests silent on the printed almanac. RD93 read 2026-10-31: 金神 in the 簠簋内伝 巻一 §5–§9 on the scans of 1632 and 1919 (year tables, 遊行, 間日), the only old text found that gives it; not in the 暦林問答集; the court diaries in the 古事類苑 (1909); today's blended year table |

| [questions/q0863.md](questions/q0863.md) | question q863 | Does «མཚན་སྟོད» mean the first part of the night up to midnight (Byang pa, 'Bum nag gsal sgron) or dusk with midnight alike (Khro ru), and is the part from midnight to dawn named anywhere in the Four Tantras' tradition? | open; cites gt:i42 |
| [questions/q0864.md](questions/q0864.md) | question q864 | Does the Co ne Svapnohana (vol. 28, 133b3–135a3, img. 270–273) read «བགྲོད་མིན» or «བཟློག་མིན», «འགྲོན་བུ» or «འགྲོན་དུ», «བཅུ་ཡི་བར» or «བཅུ་པི་བར», «ག་ཡ་ཏྲི» or «གས་ཏྲི», and does its fourth-watch line end «ཟླ་ཕྱེད་པར་དུ» or «ཟླ་ཕྱེད་བར་དུ»? | open; cites svo:p271g |
| [questions/q0865.md](questions/q0865.md) | question q865 | What do the watch lines and «ལ་མོ»/«བླ་མོ» of Advayavajra's Svapnekṣā read in the Co ne (vol. 49, 130a1–130b7, img. 263–264)? | answered; cites svo:p271g, svi:p263 |
| [questions/q0866.md](questions/q0866.md) | question q866 | Which book is the puṇyakāṇḍa of the Kāṇva branch in which the Brahmavaivarta says the good dream is set out? | open; cites skd:p470-471 |
| [questions/q0867.md](questions/q0867.md) | question q867 | Is the Vāgbhaṭa Śārīrasthāna 6 passage in the Śabdakalpadruma the Aṣṭāṅgahṛdaya's, and what does its line on the early night and the cows' path say in an edition? | answered; cites skd:p471-472 |
| [questions/q0868.md](questions/q0868.md) | question q868 | Does the Bṛhaspati Svapnādhyāya (Penn Ms. Coll. 390 item 1871; Negelein's i, Leipzig 1178) read three fortnights and a month as Negelein prints it, or eight months, three months, half a month? | answered; cites neg:p14-16, pn1871:i4 |
| [questions/q0869.md](questions/q0869.md) | question q869 | Is the Brahmavaivarta's dream chapter older than the Svapnohana's Tibetan translation of about 1200, and does Scheuermann name a Sanskrit parallel? | open; cites svo:p271f |
| [questions/q0870.md](questions/q0870.md) | question q870 | In the Co ne Svapnohana the fourth-watch line stands after «ཐུན་གཉིས/གསུམ་གྱི་བར»: does it read «ཐུན་བཞི་ཟླ་བ་ཕྱེད་བར་དུ» as the Narthang prints, «ཟླ་བ་ཕྱེད་པར་དུ» or «ཟླ་ཕྱེད་པར་དུ», and is the third watch's line there «ཐུན་གསུམ་ཟླ་བ་གསུམ་གྱི་བར» or the shorter «ཐུན་གཉིས་གསུམ་གྱི་བར»? | open; cites svo:p271g |
| [questions/q0871.md](questions/q0871.md) | question q871 | The Penn, Srinagar and Ramtek manuscripts of Bṛhaspati's Svapnādhyāya give the fourth watch a month (māsaikena), the Brahmavaivarta and the Svapnohana half a month, the Odia print a month and a half glossed fifteen days: which reading is older, and could ardhamāsena be a correction of māsena? | answered; cites svo:p271f, pn1871:i4 |
| [questions/q0872.md](questions/q0872.md) | question q872 | In Negelein's manuscript i (Leipzig 1178) 'dvitīye cāṣṭame māse triyāme ca tripakṣake' has three fortnights for the third watch: does the Leipzig manuscript itself read tripakṣake, or is it Negelein's emendation of trimāsake? | open; cites neg:p14-16 |
| [questions/q0873.md](questions/q0873.md) | question q873 | Does the Co ne print's fourth-watch line of the Svapnohana end «པར་དུ» or «པར་བྱ»? | open; cites svo:p271h |
| [questions/q0874.md](questions/q0874.md) | question q874 | Is the first segment of the Svapnohana's second-watch line «བརྒྱད་པ་ལ» (Narthang, Peking) or «བརྒྱད་བར་ལ» (Derge etext) the older reading, and which does the Dpe bsdur ma's collation give? | open; cites svo:p271h |
| [questions/q0875.md](questions/q0875.md) | question q875 | Is the string of six ornamental glyphs after the page-opening Om in the first line of LGD p. 962 (and p. 526 of the Palri print) a string of seed syllables, a mantra in ornamental letters or an invocation, and in plain letters what does it read? | open; cites lgd:p962 |
| [questions/q0876.md](questions/q0876.md) | question q876 | Is «མཉམ་པོར་འོང་བའི་བདུད» the demon of self-clinging that Chöd teaches as the co-born demon (ལྷན་ཅིག་སྐྱེས་པའི་བདུད), or an attendant adversary of the person set beside «ལྷན་ཅིག་སྐྱེས་པའི་ལྷ»? | open; cites lgd:p970 |
| [questions/q0877.md](questions/q0877.md) | question q877 | Who is the monk Sgra tshad pa (དགེ་སློང་སྒྲ་ཚད་པ) whose notes close MDP's rebirth-seeing rite on p. 137, and is the Rga lo of its lineage the translator Rga lo tsā ba? | open; cites mdp:p137b |
| [questions/q0878.md](questions/q0878.md) | question q878 | Is the section numbered «gsum» at the head of the other TDR print's p. 337 (printed p. 309) the third of a series of dream-examination sets in the 'Brong rtse be'u bum, and what do the earlier numbered sections hold? | answered; cites tdr:p337-345e |
| [questions/q0879.md](questions/q0879.md) | question q879 | The etext heading names Śramaṇā, Śākyamuni and Tārā as three (gsum), yet the lion-faced dakini's rite after White Tārā is numbered «bzhi pa»: is it the fourth of the same set of four rites or a new section whose number was carried on? | answered; cites tdr:p343h |
| [questions/q0880.md](questions/q0880.md) | question q880 | What is «Dpal rno bkra», and why is this part of the Gnam chos so called? | answered; cites gnc.1:p558h |
| [questions/q0881.md](questions/q0881.md) | question q881 | Who or what is the «dmar mo» (the Red One, a red woman) whose cremation ashes are used in the rite on GNC p. 558? | open; cites gnc.1:p558h |
| [questions/q0882.md](questions/q0882.md) | question q882 | Is the colophon of SKP's first text (p. 50, «ཤཱཀྱའི་དགེ་སློང་ …»), read only by machine, really «ཀུན་དགའ་རྒྱལ་མཚན་དཔལ་བཟང་པོ»? | answered; cites skp:p55h |
| [questions/q0883.md](questions/q0883.md) | question q883 | Is «པདྨ་དཀར» at the end of SKP's second text (p. 55) the name Padma dkar po, and which text is «well explained»? | open; cites skp:p55h |
| [questions/q0884.md](questions/q0884.md) | question q884 | Does «ཟ་ཟི» in «རྨི་ལམ་ཟ་ཟི་མང་བ» (WB vol. 1 p. 431) have a meaning of its own (confused, restless, repeated) in the reckoners' or physicians' usage, or is it a doubled sound with the sense «many dreams» only? | answered; cites wb.1:p431b |
| [questions/q0885.md](questions/q0885.md) | question q885 | What does «ལྷས་ཚོར་དུས་ཏེ» (ch. 81, the last clause of the dream list) say: is ལྷས་ཚོར་ «the time of feeling the god (spirit)» or «when the time of feeling comes»? | open; cites st:p350-351 |
| [questions/q0886.md](questions/q0886.md) | question q886 | Does the Zhol print's སྦལ་ལྗོངས་ (a ས after ལྗོང) in ch. 81 stand on the scan, and what do the Derge and the Chag-po-ri copy read there? | open; cites st:p350-351 |
| [questions/q0887.md](questions/q0887.md) | question q887 | Does the Serthang print (printed pp. 31–32, the leaf absent from the pool's scan I3CN5038) read «བརྗེད་ངས» as all eight other prints do, or «བརྗེད་ངེས»? | open; cites gt:i42 |
| [questions/q0888.md](questions/q0888.md) | question q888 | Does any commentary of the Four Tantras' tradition give the stretch of the night from midnight to dawn a name or a rule of its own, as the three-thirds rule of LGD and RST does, or is it simply the approach of «ཐོ་རངས»? | answered; cites gt:i42 |
| [questions/q0889.md](questions/q0889.md) | question q889 | Is the third syllable of the Gāyatrī's first word in the Svapnohana «ཏྲི» (Peking) or «ཏྲེ» (Yigdzin-1 on the Narthang strip), and is the word the Sanskrit gāyatrī rendered «ག་ཡ་ཏྲི»? | open; cites svo:p271i |
| [questions/q0890.md](questions/q0890.md) | question q890 | What is the Sanskrit title of the Svapnohana, «སྭ…བྣ/པྣ…ཨུ་ཧཱ་ནཱ»: which stacks stand second and third on the Narthang manuscript (img. 284 line 2) and in the Peking print, and is it svapnoha or svapnoddhāra? | open; cites svo:p271i |
| [questions/q0891.md](questions/q0891.md) | question q891 | Does the cows' line of the Aṣṭāṅgahṛdaya (Śārīrasthāna 6.63) read gosarge in the older manuscripts too, or gomārge as in some prints (the Śabdakalpadruma's gomarge)? | open; cites skd:p471-472 |
| [questions/q0892.md](questions/q0892.md) | question q892 | Is the afternoon for the bright beginning in the Aṅgavijjā ch. 41 a scribe's slip for the forenoon, as the lists of chs. 42 and 53 suggest, or a variant of the text itself? | open; cites svo:p271f |
| [questions/q0893.md](questions/q0893.md) | question q893 | Is the last letter of «ལྷས་ཚོར» in ch. 81 on the Chag-po-ri print (I1KG13487 img. 426, line 6) a ར or a ང (Yigdzin-1 reads ལྷས་ཚོང)? | answered; cites st:p350-351, st:p350-351b |
| [questions/q0894.md](questions/q0894.md) | question q894 | Is the Serthang print's own book (the Larung Gar typeset edition of the Four Tantras, 611 pages) held complete in a library whose scan BDRC lacks, so that printed pp. 31–32 could be read for «བརྗེད་ངས» and «མི་བཟང་བ»? | open; cites gt:i42 |
| [questions/q0895.md](questions/q0895.md) | question q895 | On the 1996 print p. 144 l. 13 the last syllable of the riding animals is too small to tell «རྟ» from «ཧ» by eye: is a scan of the 1996 print at higher resolution to be had? | open; cites wb.2:p144r |
| [questions/q0896.md](questions/q0896.md) | question q896 | In ch. 91's remedies for a spirit that leads the sleeper away in his dream, what does ཕྱྭ (phywa) mean beside མོ (mo, divination)? | answered; cites st:p493, st:p350-351c |
| [questions/q0897.md](questions/q0897.md) | question q897 | On a sharper scan of the Zhol print img. 675 line 6 (or the Chag-po-ri print), what do the small letters after «མོ་ཕྱྭ་རིམ་གྲ» say, and do they agree with the Serthang's two bracketed remedies word for word? | open; cites st:p493 |
| [questions/q0898.md](questions/q0898.md) | question q898 | On the Narthang manuscript's Amoghasiddhi line (I2KG212330 img. 290, end of line 2), what is the lower sign of the second syllable and what follows «ཏེ»: is the bird «བྱ་ཤིང་ཏེའུ» as Peking's machine reading and the Dpe bsdur ma note's «ཤང་ཏེའུ», or does the manuscript carry a fourth syllable as the Derge etext's «རུ»? | open; cites svi:p289-291b |
| [questions/q0899.md](questions/q0899.md) | question q899 | The Narthang manuscript and the Peking print share «ཛ་ཏ་པ» and «སྭ་པ་ནེ་ཀྵ» against the Derge etext: is the Derge etext's «ཛཱ་ཏ་ཀ» (jātaka) a correction by the Derge editors, or does the Sanskrit of the Peking line stand for another word (jātapa)? | open; cites svi:p289-291b |
| [questions/q0900.md](questions/q0900.md) | question q900 | Does the Blue Beryl's commentary on the Explanatory Tantra (Vaidūrya sngon po, part 2) say anything on the Four Tantras' own two night lines beyond the Moonbeams' gloss read at etext p. 209 (BDRC I1PD411)? | answered; cites gt:i42 |
| [questions/q0901.md](questions/q0901.md) | question q901 | Is Bya mdo Klu byams rgyal's quotation of «gsum cha phyis 'byung gsal byed yin» beside the Four Tantras' two lines (BDRC MW8LS18598, search snippet only) a joining of two rules of the same night, as the modern Tibetan books do, or does he read them as one? | open; cites gt:i42 |
| [questions/q0902.md](questions/q0902.md) | question q902 | In the Blue Beryl's ch. 7 the first part of the night is up to midnight on the Eight Limbs' verse (etext p. 209) and dusk with midnight alike on the Four Tantras' verse (p. 216): is this one rule read twice, or two different spans? | answered; cites gt:i42 |
| [questions/q0903.md](questions/q0903.md) | question q903 | Does the Moonbeams' gloss of the Eight Limbs' «རྨི་ལམ་སྐྱེས་པ་མཐོང» (a man and an elephant) belong to ch. 2's line, or only to the Eight Limbs' text that the Blue Beryl quotes beside it? | answered; cites st:p21 |
| [questions/q0904.md](questions/q0904.md) | question q904 | Where the Moonbeams on the Heart of Medicine (Aṣṭāṅgahṛdaya, Body ch. 6) read «མི་ཟད་རྨི་ལམ་» in the verse Rin spungs pa quotes, do the Derge and Peking-type Tengyur etexts (MW23703_4312, MW1PD95844_3549) spell it so too, and does Byang pa's lemma of the Four Tantras' line 17 follow the Tantra or the Moonbeams? | answered; cites gt:i42 |
| [questions/q0905.md](questions/q0905.md) | question q905 | Is the Drikung dka' mchan's wording «མི་བཟང་བ» at p. 80 a lemma of the verse or only the commentator's gloss? | answered; cites gt:i42 |
| [questions/q0906.md](questions/q0906.md) | question q906 | On the Derge, Narthang and Peking prints, is the bird of Amoghasiddhi's signs «བྱ་ཤར་ཏེ་རུ», «བྱ་ཤིང་ཏེའུ» or «བྱ་ཤང་ཏེའུ», and is it a bird sent as messenger or something else? | open; cites svi:p310 |
| [questions/q0907.md](questions/q0907.md) | question q907 | «བགྲོད་མིན་ལ་ཞུགས་ཤིས་པའོ» closes a list of ill signs: does it say that entering a path not to be walked is auspicious, or that the signs just listed are auspicious when one is not walking, and is there a commentary on the Svapnohana that glosses it? | open; cites svo:p271k |
| [questions/q0908.md](questions/q0908.md) | question q908 | LGD p. 970 names «ལྷན་ཅིག་སྐྱེས་པའི་ལྷ» and «མཉམ་པོར་འོང་བའི་བདུད» among the causes of a confused dream; the Chöd texts gloss the second as one's own co-born ignorance and self-clinging (ལས་གདོན, las gdon), the sermon on death as the keeper of the black pebbles: which does LGD's author mean, or does he not distinguish them? | open; cites lgd:p970 |
| [questions/q0909.md](questions/q0909.md) | question q909 | What is «རམ་རུག» (ram rug) in the rites' lists: the plant ram bu or ram pa by a spelling by ear, or a separate item, and what does «ཞབས» in «ཞབས་རམ་རུག» of the Rin chen gter mdzod's second text add? | open; cites lgd.42:p969b |
| [questions/q0910.md](questions/q0910.md) | question q910 | Does the Blue Beryl's printed page read ལྷས or ལྟས in the closing clause of ch. 81's dream list, and ནེའུ་གསིང as its etext has it? | open; cites st:p350-351 |
| [questions/q0911.md](questions/q0911.md) | question q911 | Is the Blue Beryl's gloss of ch. 81's dream list the same text as Khro ru's, and which came first? | answered; cites st:p350-351 |
| [questions/q0912.md](questions/q0912.md) | question q912 | On the Narthang manuscript (I2KG212330 img. 290, end of line 2) is the bird «བྱ་ཤང་ཏེའུ» as the Dpe bsdur ma note gives it, or «བྱ་ཤང་ཤང་ཏེའུ» with the full name? | open; cites svi:p310b |
| [questions/q0913.md](questions/q0913.md) | question q913 | Is the sign of Amoghasiddhi's family «ཤང་ཤང་ཏེའུ» (shang shang te'u, the jīvajīvaka bird of the lists), and what does a bird of that name sent as a messenger (ཕོ་ཉར་གཏོང) foretell in the commentaries of the tradition? | open; cites svi:p310b, svi:p261b |
| [questions/q0914.md](questions/q0914.md) | question q914 | What exactly do the notes say on the Derge (img. 605 last line, img. 606 first line) and the Chag-po-ri (img. 603 first line) prints, letter by letter, and does the Chag-po-ri set the whole run in smaller type? | open; cites st:p493b |
| [questions/q0915.md](questions/q0915.md) | question q915 | What is བ་ར (ba ra) in «བ་རའི་འོ་མ», a milk of which animal, and why is the milk with dung rubbed on the soles for a spirit's dream? | open; cites st:p493b, st:p493c |
| [questions/q0916.md](questions/q0916.md) | question q916 | Does the 星槎ラボ 延宝2 大ざつしよ, the earliest print, or any other 万代大雑書 print set out the table 夢を見て語らざる日 with a legend for the slash? | open; cites mdz:p11 |
| [questions/q0917.md](questions/q0917.md) | question q917 | In which Edo source is the 初夢 night fixed at 2 January, and is it counted by the lunar months of the Edo calendar, the only one in use until 1872? | open; cites nip:p1 |
| [questions/q0918.md](questions/q0918.md) | question q918 | On the Derge print the word before «ལྟོ་འཕྱེ» reads «བླ་མོ» by the two loops of its stack, against the plain ལ of the Peking and the Narthang prints: is the subjoined ླ a real variant of the Derge tradition, and does a reader of the tradition know the word as «རྟ་བླ་མོ» (the centipede) in a list of creatures? | open; cites svo:p271l |
| [questions/q0919.md](questions/q0919.md) | question q919 | In Puṇyavijaya's ch. 41 (p. 185 line 28), do the manuscripts Haṃ and Ta read «avaraṇhesu» for the bright beginning as the print has it, and do the others read «puvvaṇhesu» without the editor noting it? | open; cites agv:p185 |
| [questions/q0920.md](questions/q0920.md) | question q920 | Does the Leipzig manuscript 1178 read «tripakṣake», «trimāsake» or another word for the third watch, and the Ramtek M 856 «trimāsake»? | open; cites rmk:i2b |
| [questions/q0921.md](questions/q0921.md) | question q921 | Do the older manuscripts of the Aṣṭāṅgahṛdaya all read «gosarge» at 6.63, and is the Saṅgraha's «sadyaḥ» against the Hṛdaya's «tadahaḥ» a difference of the two works or of their copies? | open; cites ahr:p436 |
| [questions/q0922.md](questions/q0922.md) | question q922 | What does the Moonbeams' «མཚན་སྟོད་ཕྱེད་པ་ཡན་ཆད» mean: up to the half of the first part of the night, or up to the half (midnight) of the night? | open; cites st:p31-32b |
| [questions/q0923.md](questions/q0923.md) | question q923 | Which tantra do Rang byung rdo rje and Kong sprul mean by «rgyud rnams las», and the Blue Beryl and Zur mkhar ba by «རྒྱུད་ནས་མང་དུ་གསུངས་པ», for the verse «སྲོད་ལ་བད་ཀན་ཤས་ཆེ་སྟེ། ནམ་ཕྱེད་དུས་སུ་མཁྲིས་པར་བརྗོད། ཐོ་རངས་རླུང་གི་དུས་ཉིད་དེ»? | open; cites st:p31-32c |
| [questions/q0924.md](questions/q0924.md) | question q924 | Do the two editions of Byang pa's commentary (MW27496, the Si khron print of 2001, and MW15590, the Mi rigs dpe skrun khang first printing) differ on the page in the Moonbeams' line (མི་བཟང་ against མི་བཟད), and which is the author's own? | open; cites st:p30-31 |
| [questions/q0925.md](questions/q0925.md) | question q925 | Does Bya mdo Klu byams rgyal join the Klong rdol line and the Four Tantras' first line into one rule (dusk and midnight without fruit, dawn with), or only set them side by side as two authorities? | open; cites st:p31-32c |
| [questions/q0926.md](questions/q0926.md) | question q926 | In the Co ne print the second watch's first segment machine-reads «བརྒྱད་པར་» with a ར and the fourth watch's «ཕྱེད་པར་» likewise: is «པར» a variant of the Co ne tradition, and does the Dpe bsdur ma note it? | open; cites svo:p271m |
| [questions/q0927.md](questions/q0927.md) | question q927 | The Co ne's third watch line reads «ཐུན་གཉིས་གསུམ་གྱི་བར» (no «ཟླ་བ», «གཉིས» for «གསུམ»): is it the scribe's slip or the print's own text? | open; cites svo:p271m |
| [questions/q0928.md](questions/q0928.md) | question q928 | Does the Sanskrit of Nīlakaṇṭha's Ācāramayūkha (Negelein's q, Benares 1876 with Rāmanātha's Hindi commentary) read the fourth watch «māsena phalada» and the dawn «daśāhena» in a printed Sanskrit edition, as the Romanian translation's text layer shows? | open; cites neg:p14-17 |
| [questions/q0929.md](questions/q0929.md) | question q929 | Can a reader of the manuscript hand confirm the colophon of SKP's first text (p. 50, lines 3–4) as «ཤཱཀྱའི་དགེ་སློང་ཀུན་དགའ་རྒྱལ་མཚན་དཔལ་བཟང་པོ» and tell «གསུམ་པ» from «གསུངས་པ» in the second text's opening line? | open; cites skp:p50 |
| [questions/q0930.md](questions/q0930.md) | question q930 | Is «ལེགས་པར་བཤད་པ་པདྨ་དཀར» at the end of SKP's second text a title or the signature of a person, and does the second text close so in the Mongolian copy NLM9419 and in MDP's copy (p. 138 ff.), whose end was not read? | open; cites skp:p55 |
| [questions/q0931.md](questions/q0931.md) | question q931 | Is the «Rga lo» of the lineage on MDP p. 135 the translator Rga lo tsā ba, or a misreading of «G.yag sde lo tsā ba» (Bsod nams bzang po), whom three Sa skya lineages name as the teacher of Ze dmar ba Sangs rgyas ye shes? | open; cites mdp:p135 |
| [questions/q0932.md](questions/q0932.md) | question q932 | Is the monk Sgra tshad pa of MDP p. 137 Rin chen rnam rgyal, Bu ston's pupil and biographer, whom BDRC's records call «dge slong sgra tshad pa»? | open; cites mdp:p137b |
| [questions/q0933.md](questions/q0933.md) | question q933 | Who wrote or compiled the three dream examinations of the third section (Śramaṇā, Śākyamuni, Tārā) in the 'Brong rtse be'u bum? | open; cites tdr:p21e |
| [questions/q0934.md](questions/q0934.md) | question q934 | On the Kazi print (I6132 img. 559) and the Zhe chen and Ngodrup hands, is the letter after «མ» in the mixed kind's last clause ཕ or བ, that is «མ་ཕྱེད་ན» or «མ་བྱེད་ན»? | open; cites lgd.42:p966-971c |
| [questions/q0935.md](questions/q0935.md) | question q935 | In the middling yogin's line did the Lama Gongdü read «རང་བཞིན་གྱིས་ལ་ཉེ་བརྟག» (Palri and Kazi) or «རང་གཞན་ལ་ཉེ་བརྟགས» (the Rin chen gter mdzod and Shechen)? | open; cites lgd.42:p966-971c |
| [questions/q0936.md](questions/q0936.md) | question q936 | The Four Tantras' line about the spirit entering has the same «རྨི་ལམ་ཟ་ཟི་མང»; Khro ru adds a rite there (I5622 p. 341) and treats it as the spirit's sign, against the fever places (I5621 p. 120): is the dream the sick one's symptom or the spirit's sign, and does the Tantra mean one by both lines? | answered; cites st:p183, st:p350-351 |
| [questions/q0937.md](questions/q0937.md) | question q937 | Is ཟ་ཟི་ནི་ཉག་ཉོག in the Lhan thabs's life-wind chapter the same word as in the fevers, or a term in a list? | open; cites st:p183 |
| [questions/q0938.md](questions/q0938.md) | question q938 | Who wrote the Sakya Vinaya treatise that places the dawn against the night's watches, and is its last clause on the nine parts the etext's error? | answered; cites svo:p271p |
| [questions/q0939.md](questions/q0939.md) | question q939 | Do the commentaries on WB say that WB's eight watches of the day and night are the tantric ones of the Muktāvalī and Rang byung rdo rje? | open; cites svo:p271o |
| [questions/q0940.md](questions/q0940.md) | question q940 | Does «སྙིང་སྡུག» in the Svapnohana mean something dear to the heart or heartache? | answered; cites svo:p271n |
| [questions/q0941.md](questions/q0941.md) | question q941 | Is «ཐུན་གསུམ» the Tibetan of Sanskrit triyāmā, and is it in the Amarakośa? | answered; cites svo:p271p |
| [questions/q0942.md](questions/q0942.md) | question q942 | On KCM img. 691 line 5 does the fruit of the rite end «…སྲིད་གསུམ་མཐོང་» (the three existences seen) or does the line name something else («ཁམས་སུ་མཆོད»)? | open; cites kcm:p474 |
| [questions/q0943.md](questions/q0943.md) | question q943 | SKP's line «ཁ་ནས་སོ་ནི་འབུད་གྱུར་ན» (p. 50), read as vomiting, reads letter by letter 'if teeth are thrown out of the mouth': which is it, and does it then match WB's «སོ་གོག» (teeth falling)? | open; cites wb.2:p144l |
| [questions/q0944.md](questions/q0944.md) | question q944 | Does WB's dream list come from the stock of dream images that Klong rdol's verses (given from the Chöd collection of Ma gcig) and the 'Bri gung method also draw on, or from a text not yet in the pool? | open; cites wb.2:p144l |
| [questions/q0945.md](questions/q0945.md) | question q945 | How does the night's name ཐུན་གསུམ་པ relate to the Mahāvyutpatti's third watch of five? | open; cites svo:p271q |
| [questions/q0946.md](questions/q0946.md) | question q946 | Are the Dbyangs 'char's eight half-watches of the day-and-night equal? | answered; cites svo:p271r |
| [questions/q0947.md](questions/q0947.md) | question q947 | Who wrote the Rnam dpyod blo gros spyan stong bkra ba, and which Dbyangs 'char verse does it quote? | answered; cites svo:p271r |
| [questions/q0948.md](questions/q0948.md) | question q948 | Does the Derge, Chag-po-ri or Drathang print of ch. 81's verse on the ways of entering carry a clause on the dream in the second way's line, as the commentaries' glosses do, or is the Serthang's bare list of five the Tantra's text? | open; cites st:p352 |
| [questions/q0949.md](questions/q0949.md) | question q949 | Did Sde srid build his gloss of the five ways of entering on the 'Bri gung dka' mchan, whose verse-lines carry the same clauses in the same order, or on a source both used? | open; cites st:p352b |
| [questions/q0950.md](questions/q0950.md) | question q950 | Does the Blue Beryl's printed page (the Dpe tshogs print, p. 1148 for the dream list) read ལྷས or ལྟས in «ལྟས་ཚོར་བའི་དུས», as Khro ru's page reads ལྷས? | open; cites st:p352b |
| [questions/q0951.md](questions/q0951.md) | question q951 | Does the Dpe bsdur ma's base text read «བརྒྱད་པར་ལ» and «ཕྱེད་པར་དུ» (with པ) in the second and the fourth watch lines, as entries 4 and 6 of printed p. 1301 imply, or «བརྒྱད་བར་ལ» and «ཕྱེད་བར་དུ» as the OCR gives them? | open; cites svo:p271u |
| [questions/q0952.md](questions/q0952.md) | question q952 | Does the Dpe bsdur ma name the Co ne in every place where the Co ne differs from the Derge base (its siglum is in about 120 entries of vol. 14), so that its silence at the watch lines, «བགྲོད་མིན», «འགྲོན་བུ», «རྟ་བླ་མོ» and the Gāyatrī means the Co ne agrees with the base? | open; cites svo:p271u |
| [questions/q0953.md](questions/q0953.md) | question q953 | In the Svapnekṣā did the editors replace «ཤར་ཏེ་རུ» or «བྱ་ཤར་ཏེ་རུ» by «ཤང་ཏེའུ» for the Narthang (and by «ཤིང་ཏེའུ» for the Peking)? (the lemma of entry 4 of printed p. 355) | open; cites svo:p271v |
| [questions/q0954.md](questions/q0954.md) | question q954 | The Mongolian manuscript has no author's name in the part that survives, is undated, and the catalogue gives no date for its volume of the Dga' ldan Byang rtse college's collection: is the text older than Klong rdol bla ma (1719–1794), and is the Mother's Chöd collection a real book that holds these verses or only the authority the verses name? | open; cites gdm:i88-93 |
| [questions/q0955.md](questions/q0955.md) | question q955 | The manuscript and KBS read «སུམ་ཆར» and «ཐོ་རངས་དུས་ཀྱི་ནམ་གྱི་གསུམ་ཆ་ལ», RST «གསུམ་ཆ» and «ཐོ་རངས་ཁ་ཡིན་ནམ་གྱི་གསུམ་ཆ་ལ»: which is the earlier wording, and which print did the KBS article use? | open; cites gdm:i88-93 |
| [questions/q0956.md](questions/q0956.md) | question q956 | The unsaddled donkey, «རྟ་བོང་རྒ་མེད་ཞོན» in the manuscript (machine) against «སྒ་མེད» in RST, KBS and WB: is the manuscript's first letter «རྒ» (old age) or «སྒ» (a saddle)? | open; cites gdm:i88-93 |
| [questions/q0957.md](questions/q0957.md) | question q957 | In the Gtsang sman text the physician's dawn dream is the first of thirteen examinations of the sick (etext p. 263): does this arrangement, and the rule that the physician declines the case after a bad dream, rest on the Four Tantras' own chapter on examining the sick or on the Siddhasāra's chapter 5? | answered; cites gsm:p263-264, sds:p75-77 |
| [questions/q0958.md](questions/q0958.md) | question q958 | The Siddhasāra's chapter 5 ends «འཆི་ལྟས་ཀྱི་ལེའུ་སྟེ་དྲུག་ལྔ །པ་རྫོགས་སོ།» (etext p. 77, where both numerals stand), while the contents number the chapter fifth: is sixth the older count, a chapter having been split, or a slip of the print? | answered; cites sds:p75-77 |
| [questions/q0959.md](questions/q0959.md) | question q959 | What do the first small-type note's letters read on the Derge print of ch. 91 (img. 605, last line, after «རྟེན་འབྲེལ་བ»): «རང་རེ་འོས་ས་སྦྱར་བ», a milk, or a third wording, on a sharper image of the Derge than the BDRC microfilm? | open; cites st:p493d |
| [questions/q0960.md](questions/q0960.md) | question q960 | Does the Drathang print of ch. 81 carry a clause on the dream in the second way's line, as the Serthang, Derge and Chag-po-ri do not? | answered; cites st:p352c, st:p350-351c |
| [questions/q0961.md](questions/q0961.md) | question q961 | On the Peking print (I1KG13215 img. 310 line 1) the Svapnekṣā's last part of the night reads either «ཐ་མར་རྨི་ལམ་བཏབ་ལ་ཉལ» (the Dpe bsdur ma's Peking and Narthang entry, and eight of eight Yigdzin-1 runs) or «ཐ་མར་རྨི་ལམ་བརྟགས་པ་ལ་ཉལ» (the eye on three readings): is there a sharper image of this leaf, or a reader of the Peking's hand who can say how many syllables stand between «ལམ» and «ལ»? | open; cites svo:p271w |
| [questions/q0962.md](questions/q0962.md) | question q962 | Does the Dpe bsdur ma, in the Svapnohana's third watch line, leave «བར» against «བར་དུ» unrecorded because the Peking and the Narthang differ from the base there, or because the base reads «བར་དུ» too? | open; cites svo:p271w |
| [questions/q0963.md](questions/q0963.md) | question q963 | Bu ston's text ends with a lineage that names «Jam gsar» and a lo tsā ba whom the machine reads «རྒ་ལོ»; which masters are meant, and is the rite the one BDRC's title «རྨི་ལམ་བརྟག་པ» means, the first syllable of the title line on img. 833 «རྟག» or «བརྟག»? | open; cites bsr:p825-828 |
| [questions/q0964.md](questions/q0964.md) | question q964 | In the Tsong kha pa passage the first three parts of the night are called uncertain and the fourth taken, with a verse of the Mañjuśrī root tantra giving phlegm, bile and wind: is this the division by which the Tibetan medical dream texts (SDS, GSM) judge a dream, or a tantric one only? | open; cites tkg:i385-388 |
| [questions/q0965.md](questions/q0965.md) | question q965 | Which colours of light go with humans and demi-gods in Bu ston's rite (the machine reads green for humans and yellow for demi-gods)? | open; cites bsr:p825-828 |
| [questions/q0966.md](questions/q0966.md) | question q966 | In WB vol. 2 p. 403, are the eight watches (ཐུན་བརྒྱད) of the day-and-night the eight half-watches of the day together with the eight of the night (sixteen in all), as the later books count the Dbyangs 'char's half-watches, or eight in all? | open; cites svo:p271w |
| [questions/q0967.md](questions/q0967.md) | question q967 | Which text is the «Dbyangs 'char text» (དབྱངས་འཆར་གཞུང) that the Rnam dpyod quotes (the Rgyus 'grel Mchog dga' is a commentary on it): is the verse in it, or in a commentary? | open; cites svo:p271x |
| [questions/q0968.md](questions/q0968.md) | question q968 | The Kun 'dus chen mo, from the Mchog dga', gives each of the eight half-watches four chu tshod; eight times four is thirty-two and the day-time at an equinox has thirty: is the four a round figure, or are the half-watches of the Mchog dga' reckoned otherwise? | answered; cites svo:p271y |
| [questions/q0969.md](questions/q0969.md) | question q969 | The copy in W1NLM9044 part 30 writes the number of the thirds of the night as a numeral and ends with Klong rdol bla ma's colophon and a second writer's («ཅེས་པ་འདི་སྔ་སུམ་པ་མཁན་པོས་བྲིས་པའོ»): who is the writer of the second colophon, and does the numeral stand for «སུམ» or «གསུམ»? | open; cites gdn:i262 |
| [questions/q0970.md](questions/q0970.md) | question q970 | The unsaddled donkey is «སྒ་མེད» in KBS, RST, WB and (by eye) GDR but «རྒ་མེད» in the machine reading of GDM and GDN: is the scribe's letter a ra-head or a sa-head, and does a reader of the hand know the contraction? | open; cites gdn:i252-254, gdr:i151-157 |
| [questions/q0971.md](questions/q0971.md) | question q971 | Is the green of the six lights ལྗང་ཁུ (Zhol par khang print) or ལྡང་ཁུ (the Delhi print's machine reading), and what are the first-verse mantra's syllables in Bu ston's rite? | open; cites bsr:p599-603, bsr:p617-621 |
| [questions/q0972.md](questions/q0972.md) | question q972 | Do the square brackets at the end of the Siddhasāra's fevers chapter on the print (etext p. 77, the «ལྔ») stand on the page as at pp. 72 and 89, and which order of chapters 4 and 5 (signs of death fourth, as the opening list, or fevers fourth, as the contents) is older? | open; cites sds:p72-73, sds:p27 |
| [questions/q0973.md](questions/q0973.md) | question q973 | How does the Moonbeams read the good messenger «ya mtshan can» in the Four Tantras' ch. 7, and from what source does Gtsang sman take the physician's dawn dream as the first of his examinations? | open; cites gt:i41, gsm:p263-264 |
| [questions/q0974.md](questions/q0974.md) | question q974 | In the lama's remark in the dream verses (cbl:p236-238) the etext reads «འོ་ཡུག་པ་གཤིན་རྗེ་དུས་མཚན་ནག་མོ་ཡིན་གསུང་སྐད» after the saying that the black girl's binding is the sign of the time of death's arrival: who or what is the «འོ་ཡུག་པ», and is the remark the old annotator's or Mkhyen brtse's? | open; cites cbl:p236-238 |
| [questions/q0975.md](questions/q0975.md) | question q975 | In the verse on the outer signs the Derge reads «ལོ་བརྒྱད» (eight years) and CBL «ལོ་ནི་བརྒྱར» (a hundred years): which is the Indian reading, and which print of the Derge blocks has which? | open; cites cbs:p247-249 |
| [questions/q0976.md](questions/q0976.md) | question q976 | In the list of the works the treatise rests on (cbs:p267-268, cbl:p278-279) which works are the «བླ་གབ», the «དུས་ཀྱི་ཕྱི་མ» and the «དུས་ཕྲེང་བ», and is the Fifty on Life the Nāgārjuna work that CBL says it is? | open; cites cbs:p267-268, cbl:p278-279 |
| [questions/q0977.md](questions/q0977.md) | question q977 | Are WB's eight watches on p. 403 («ཉིན་མཚན་ཐུན་བརྒྱད») eight of the day and night together, or eight of the day with a further eight of the night? Its division line reads «ཉིན་ཚད», its first line «ཉིན་མཚན». | open; cites wb.2:p403c |
| [questions/q0978.md](questions/q0978.md) | question q978 | On p. 375, the summer's first stretch «དབྱར་གསུམ་ཉི་གོང[?]་ནམ་ཕྱེད་བར»: is the second word «ཉི་གོང» as Yigdzin-1 has it, or «ཉི་ནུབ» as in spring and autumn? | open; cites wb.1:p375 |
| [questions/q0979.md](questions/q0979.md) | question q979 | On p. 403, line 5, «མིག་དམར་འཇོག[?]»: is the last letter ཇ (the earlier passages) or ཛ (Yigdzin-1)? | open; cites wb.2:p403c |
| [questions/q0980.md](questions/q0980.md) | question q980 | Which reign or era is «སྲིད་གསལ» in the dated colophon of the 10th text of W1NLM9044 (the ninth year, an ox year, fifteenth of the month Sa ga), and who is the mantra-madman Padma 'od zer? | open; cites gdn:i175 |
| [questions/q0981.md](questions/q0981.md) | question q981 | Is the writer of the copies, Blo bzang karma bkra shis of the Red-Hat family (GDM's volume, 2nd text) or Blo bzang nyi ma (GDR's volume), the same hand as the dream text's, and can a reader of Mongolian manuscript hands date the three volumes by paper and hand? | open; cites gdm:i82, gdr:i37 |
| [questions/q0982.md](questions/q0982.md) | question q982 | In the Sha za brtsegs pa'i rgyud's ninth section the signs of nearness (black man, black dog, raven, tiger, thunder) stand under the title «the chapter of the dream signs»: are they dreams of the practitioner at the approach, and are the lines after «ཐོག་འཚག་ཉེ་བའི་མཚན་མའོ» a second group, signs of the work succeeding? | open; cites szb:i463 |
| [questions/q0983.md](questions/q0983.md) | question q983 | Does Dbang chen dgyes rab rdo rje follow a text for the dream step (he cites the Mantra Stages, «སྔགས་རིམ་ཆེན་མོ»), and does the Vairocana initiation tradition name signs a teacher reads in the pupils' dawn dreams? | answered; cites dgd:i80-82, snr:p349 |
| [questions/q0984.md](questions/q0984.md) | question q984 | Is the 夢合長者鑑, a dream book listed at forty sen in the publisher's list of the 万代大雑書三世相 (Suzuki Kiemon, 1886), set out in the NDL or any library, and does it carry the table of the days on which a dream is not told with a legend for the slash, or a 初夢 night? | open; cites mdz:i190 |
| [questions/q0985.md](questions/q0985.md) | question q985 | On the Derge print (I1344 img. 248–249) are the letters of «དྲི་དམར», «ལོ་བརྒྱད» and «ཀོ་པི་ད་ར» (Yigdzin-1 «ཀོ་བི་དར») those of the Derge etext, or does the eye's pa/ba and da/ra reading differ on a sharper image? | open; cites cbs:p248-249s |
| [questions/q0986.md](questions/q0986.md) | question q986 | Does the Dbyangs 'char verse, the Gso rig gces btus piece and WB vol. 2 p. 403's «ཆུ་སྲང་བཞི་རེ» use the same 56¼-chu-srang unit as the Mchog dga'? | open; cites svo:p271z |

| [questions/q0990.md](questions/q0990.md) | question q990 | The 夢合延寿袋大成 (1814, 夢占道人), the 夢合判断大成 (1830) and the 清明秘鈔夢判事 (1862), dream books listed in the catalogue without a digitised copy at the NDL, may be held open at another library (Waseda, the University of Tokyo, a university's collection): does any of them set out the table of the days on which a dream is not told, with a legend for the slash, or a 初夢 night and its calendar? | open; cites mdz:i899567 |

| [questions/q0994.md](questions/q0994.md) | question q994 | The Svapnohana's title stands as «སྭ…ཨུ་ཧཱ་ནཱ» on the Peking and the Narthang with a box-headed second stack («པྣ» or «བྣ»); is the Sanskrit svapnoha or svapnoddhāra, and does a Sanskritist know the work under a name that fixes the stack? | open; cites svo:p271a |

| [questions/q1000.md](questions/q1000.md) | question q1000 | The 雑書夢合 / 三世相小鑑 (Koizumi DIG-ORMK-02344, image 27) and the 清明秘鈔夢判事 (image 34) each carry a table of day branches in seal-script heads with a cell of cursive under each; read the cells letter by letter at 3000 px: is each cell the charm for a bad dream seen on that day, the same in both books, and does either carry the days on which a dream is not told? | open; cites mdz:i2533938 |

| [questions/q1032.md](questions/q1032.md) | question q1032 | What do the lines «རྨི་ལམ་ཞེས་བྱ་ཡོད་པ་སྤངས། བྱ་བ་དུས་ཀྱི་རིམ་པ་ཉིད། དེ་ཉིད་གཟིགས་པས་བསྟན་པ་ཡིན» (Toh 543 pp. 400-401) mean: is the dream «given up» in every way, and what is «the order of the times of the act»? | open; cites mmk.1:p400-401 |
| [questions/q1033.md](questions/q1033.md) | question q1033 | Does the Tibetan tradition hold a dream seen on the night of the tenth or fifteenth lunar day to be better, truer or more worth telling than one on another night? Nine of the thirteen hits of «ཚེས་བཅུའི་ནུབ་མོ་རྨི་ལམ» and «ཚེས་བཅོ་ལྔའི་ནུབ་མོ་རྨི་ལམ» are visions on those nights, mostly of Orgyan or a dākinī; no sentence states a rule. | open; cites dds.1:p523 |
| [questions/q1034.md](questions/q1034.md) | question q1034 | What is «མར་ངོའི་རྨི་ལམ་མང་དུས་སུ། ཞག་དགུའི་བར་དུ་གཏང་བར་བྱ» in the rite for conceiving a boy? Is «རྨི་ལམ» the dream or a misreading, and what is given for nine days? One print has «མ་དུས་», the other «མང་དུས་». | open; cites dds.12:p439 |
| [questions/q1035.md](questions/q1035.md) | question q1035 | Why is the twelfth month of the Catuṣpīṭha and Vajradākatantra list called «རྨི་ལམ་གྱི་ཟླ་བ» (the Dream month)? | [questions/q1036.md](questions/q1036.md) | question q1036 | Which text and author is the death-sign teaching «ནམ་ཕྱེད་ཀྱི་རྨི་ལམ་བརྟག་པ» (the midnight dream, with the Gya skag mountain and the naked donkey rider)? | [questions/q1038.md](questions/q1038.md) | question q1038 | In the passage about conception dreams (tsl.30:i38) what do «སུན་ཀ» and, in the diviner's words, «ལན་འདུག» mean? Is the reading «a harm» and «the place has a debt» right? | open; cites tsl.30:i36-38 |
| [questions/q1039.md](questions/q1039.md) | question q1039 | In vol. 30 i139, what does «བཅུ་གསུམ་རང་ལོ་སྐག་ཐོག་ལ» mean (the age-thirteen term with «སྐག»)? Is it a technical year or term, or a scribal mistake? | open; cites tsl.30:i138-140 |
| [questions/q1040.md](questions/q1040.md) | question q1040 | Is the tenth day on which Rtsa gsum gling pa's goddess-sister fixes his start of Dharma, and the ninth day of the Heruka dream, tied to the lunar-day series of the tenth (Guru's day) in the lineage, or only the date of that one dream? | open; cites tsl.30:i86-88 |

| [questions/q1047.md](questions/q1047.md) | question q1047 | Does the Derge's «སྡིག་པ» at the eighth place of the list of houses (mmk.1:p403) render a Sanskrit word other than «vṛścika» (the lists in ch. 1 and ch. 18 have «vṛścika»), and is «སྦལ་པ» (frog) for «karkaṭa» (crab) a variant or a slip? | open; cites mmk.1:p403-404 |
| [questions/q1048.md](questions/q1048.md) | question q1048 | In the lines «བྱ་བ་དུས་ཀྱི་རིམ་པ་ཉིད» (mmk.1:p400-401) is the Sanskrit's word «krama» (order) or «sama» (equal), and does «རྨི་ལམ་ཞེས་བྱ་ཡོད་པ་སྤངས» mean all dreams are set aside or only the «dream-named» kind? | open; cites mmk.1:p400-401 |
| [questions/q1049.md](questions/q1049.md) | question q1049 | Is the Sakya explanation that the first watch is mixed with the day, the midnight watches are deep sleep and the dawn watch is clear (Kun dga' rin chen; Rgyud sde kun btus pp. 460-461) older than him, for instance in a Sakya or Indian commentary on the Mañjuśrīmūlakalpa? | open; cites mmk.1:p397-399 |
| [questions/q1050.md](questions/q1050.md) | question q1050 | In ch. 18 of the Mañjuśrī root tantra a watch is «four chu tshod» and also «four yud tsam» while the day has «thirty yud tsam» (pp. 438-439); how are these counted, and is the dream verse's fourth watch the one between midnight and dawn, given that the omens count three watches, midnight, dawn and sunrise (pp. 436-437)? | open; cites mmk.1:p397-399 |

<!-- end pool index -->

## How the files quote

- **The scan wins.** A quotation is read on the page image. Where only the
  OCR was available, the file says so. […] marks a syllable the scan does
  not show clearly; a reading that is a guess says so.
- **The Tibetan is kept**, not only the English: each passage read is
  written out in Tibetan script as the scan shows it, the whole passage
  and not only the line used (PLAN.md, *The knowledge pool*).
- **Tibetan as printed**, spelling and the 1996 editors' bracketed numbers
  included. A normalized spelling is given next to the printed one, never
  instead of it.
- **English in our own words.** The Tibetan works are public domain;
  modern English translations (Henning, Berzin, Gyurme Dorje) are not, so
  their facts may be used but not their wording.
- **Each fact names its edition, page and image number**, so that it can be
  checked without searching.

**SN is a witness, never a source** (the owner, 2026-10-08). It may confirm
a reading of WB or raise a question about one, and a topic file may quote
it for that. Nothing that only SN gives (a reading, a list, a date, a rule
WB does not state) enters the app, its catalog or its `Sources`; the app
cites WB. The same holds for any other Bon text, among them Khyung sprul's
*dpyad gsum dag rtsis*, a reformed calendar arithmetic not used at all
([tibetastromed.md](tibetastromed.md)).

The machine-readable registry of every siglum (identifiers, volumes, page-to-image
offsets, chapters, licence, rank, role) is [sources.yaml](sources.yaml); the two
lists are kept equal by `tools/sources/kb.py check` ([STRUCTURE.md](STRUCTURE.md)).

## Numbering

Mansions are numbered as in the app's `Mansion` enum and in WB's brackets:
0 tha skar (Aśvinī) … 26 nam gru (Revatī). Weekdays as in WB's brackets:
0 spen pa (Saturday), 1 nyi ma (Sunday) … 6 pa sangs (Friday).
